> - Decipher, Oncotype, Prolaris add genomic risk.

## PARP inhibitors in Prostate cancer.
**Type:** SAQ (10 marks)  
**Source:** 2024-Winter Paper 4, Q3f

### Introduction
- **PARP (poly-ADP-ribose polymerase)** repairs single-strand DNA breaks. **PARPIs (PARP inhibitors):** olaparib, rucaparib, niraparib, talazoparib.

### Mechanism: synthetic lethality
- **Catalytic inhibition** and **PARP trapping** on DNA leave single-strand breaks unrepaired.
- At replication these become **double-strand breaks**, normally repaired by **HRR (homologous recombination repair)**.
- Tumours lacking HRR (e.g. **BRCA2** loss) die; normal cells survive.
- **AR (androgen receptor)–PARP synergy:** AR maintains HRR genes, so AR blockade adds repair deficiency: basis for PARPI + ARPI (androgen receptor pathway inhibitor).

### Who benefits: testing
- **HRR alterations in mCRPC (metastatic castration-resistant prostate cancer): up to 20–25%.** Genes: **BRCA2 (largest benefit)**, BRCA1, ATM (weak), PALB2, CHEK2, CDK12.
- **Germline testing** in all metastatic disease, strong family history or Ashkenazi ancestry; **somatic testing** on tissue or ctDNA (circulating tumour DNA), where clonal haematopoiesis can mislead.

### Key trials
| Trial | Setting | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **PROfound** | Olaparib vs enzalutamide/abiraterone after ARPI | BRCA/ATM cohort: imaging PFS (progression-free survival) **7.4 vs 3.6 months (HR [hazard ratio] 0.34)**; OS (overall survival) 19.1 vs 14.7 months in the final analysis (chapter quotes the interim 18.5 vs 15.1) |
| **TRITON3** | Rucaparib vs physician's choice | BRCA: PFS 11.2 vs 6.4 months (HR 0.50); no ATM benefit |
| **PROpel** | Olaparib + abiraterone, first-line mCRPC | OS 42.1 vs 34.7 months (P = 0.054, not significant); greatest in BRCA |
| **MAGNITUDE** | Niraparib + abiraterone, HRR-altered | BRCA rPFS (radiographic PFS) 19.5 vs 10.9 months (HR 0.55); no benefit without HRR alteration |
| **TALAPRO-2** | Talazoparib + enzalutamide, first line | All-comers rPFS not reached vs 21.9 months (HR 0.63); largest in BRCA |

- **Also know:** TOPARP-A/B, TRITON2, GALAHAD (phase II).

### Approvals (FDA [Food and Drug Administration])
- **Olaparib monotherapy:** HRR-mutated mCRPC after ARPI (2020).
- **Rucaparib:** BRCA-mutated mCRPC.
- **Olaparib or niraparib + abiraterone:** BRCA-mutated; **talazoparib + enzalutamide:** HRR-mutated (both 2023).

### Toxicity
- **Anaemia** (commonest grade 3–4; monitor counts), fatigue, nausea; rare **MDS/AML (myelodysplastic syndrome/acute myeloid leukaemia)**, pulmonary embolism.

### Recent advances
- **mCSPC (metastatic castration-sensitive prostate cancer)** trials: AMPLITUDE, TALAPRO-3; ATR inhibitors for ATM loss.

**Diagram to draw:** synthetic lethality: PARP block + BRCA loss → cell death.

> **Key points to remember:**
> - Synthetic lethality; BRCA2 gains most, ATM least.
> - PROfound: olaparib, PFS 7.4 vs 3.6 months.
> - Combinations: PROpel, MAGNITUDE, TALAPRO-2.

## PI-RADS and MRI–ultrasound fusion biopsy
**Type:** SAQ (10 marks)  
**Source:** Practice Set 4 Paper 4, Q3c
(mpMRI and PI-RADS also answered in 2024-Winter Paper 1, Q3d; biopsy in Practice Set 4 Paper 2, Q3d)

### PI-RADS
- **PI-RADS (Prostate Imaging Reporting and Data System)** v2.1 (2019) scores the likelihood of **clinically significant cancer** (Gleason ≥7, volume >0.5 cm³ or EPE [extraprostatic extension]) on mpMRI (multiparametric magnetic resonance imaging): T2W (T2-weighted: anatomy), DWI (diffusion-weighted imaging) with ADC (apparent diffusion coefficient: cellularity), DCE (dynamic contrast-enhanced: vascularity).

| Score | Significant cancer |
|---|---|
| 1 | Highly unlikely |
| 2 | Unlikely |
| 3 | Equivocal |
| 4 | Likely; <1.5 cm |
| 5 | Highly likely; ≥1.5 cm or definite EPE |

| Zone | Dominant sequence | Tie-breaker at score 3 |
|---|---|---|
| Peripheral | **DWI** | DCE positive → 4 |
| Transition | **T2W** | DWI 5 → 4 (v2.1: T2W 2 with DWI ≥4 → 3) |

- Cancer in about one-third of PI-RADS 3, over half of 4, 90% of 5.
- **Also know:** PI-QUAL (image quality); PRECISE (active surveillance).

### Fusion biopsy: principle
- Lesion contoured on MRI → software **co-registers it with live TRUS (transrectal ultrasound)** in three dimensions; **elastic deformation** corrects probe distortion → needle aimed at the displayed target.
- Freehand electromagnetic or mechanical-arm tracking; transrectal or **transperineal** (fewer infections).

| | Cognitive | Software fusion | In-bore MRI |
|---|---|---|---|
| Method | Aims from memory of MRI | Software overlay | Needle under MRI |
| Drawback | Operator-dependent; small lesion in large gland | Cost; registration error | Time, cost |

- Meta-analysis (2022): no significant difference among the three; one RCT (randomised controlled trial) favoured fusion over cognitive.

### Indications
- PI-RADS ≥3 lesion with raised PSA (prostate-specific antigen), first or repeat biopsy; active surveillance; focal therapy.
- **AUA/SUO (American Urological Association/Society of Urologic Oncology) 2023:** 2–3 cores per target ± systematic cores.

### Evidence
- **PROMIS (2017):** MRI sensitivity 93% versus 48% for TRUS biopsy; 27% avoid biopsy.
- **PRECISION (2018):** MRI pathway found more significant cancer (38% versus 26%); 28% avoided biopsy.
- Targeting reduces overdiagnosis; MRI-invisible cancers keep systematic cores relevant.

**Diagram to draw:** MRI target on live TRUS with needle path.

> **Key points to remember:**
> - Peripheral zone by DWI, transition zone by T2W; DCE only lifts peripheral 3 to 4.
> - Fusion: co-registration with elastic deformation.
> - Cognitive, fusion, in-bore: similar yield.
> - PROMIS and PRECISION: MRI before biopsy.

## Indications of genomic testing in carcinoma prostate
**Type:** SAQ (10 marks)  
**Source:** Practice Set 4 Paper 4, Q3e
(PARP [poly-ADP-ribose polymerase] inhibitors also answered in 2024-Winter Paper 4, Q3f)

### Types of test
| Type | Sample | What it finds | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Germline** | Blood or saliva | Inherited variants: BRCA2, BRCA1, ATM, CHEK2, PALB2, HOXB13, MMR (mismatch repair: MLH1, MSH2, MSH6, PMS2) | Therapy, family testing, screening |
| **Somatic** | Tumour tissue (usually primary) or ctDNA (circulating tumour DNA) | HRR (homologous recombination repair) genes, CDK12, MSI-H (microsatellite instability-high)/dMMR (MMR deficiency) | Targeted therapy |
| **Gene-expression classifiers** | Biopsy or prostatectomy tissue | Decipher (22 RNA markers); Oncotype DX GPS (Genomic Prostate Score, 17 genes); Prolaris (cell-cycle genes) | Prognosis |

### Indications: germline testing
- **All metastatic** disease (AUA [American Urological Association], NCCN [National Comprehensive Cancer Network]): over 10% carry actionable DNA-repair mutations.
- **High- or very-high-risk** localised disease.
- **Intermediate risk with intraductal or cribriform histology** (consider).
- **Family history:** early-onset or metastatic prostate cancer (relative <60 years); BRCA-related (breast, ovarian, pancreatic) or Lynch-related (colorectal, endometrial, upper tract) cancers; known familial mutation.
- **Ashkenazi Jewish ancestry.**
- **Why:** PARP inhibitor eligibility; caution with active surveillance in BRCA2; carrier screening; cascade testing of relatives with counselling.

### Indications: somatic testing
- **Metastatic disease, especially mCRPC (metastatic castration-resistant prostate cancer):** HRR alterations in up to 20–25%.
  - **HRR (BRCA2 most)** → PARP inhibitors (olaparib, rucaparib, niraparib, talazoparib).
  - **MSI-H/dMMR** (2–5%) → pembrolizumab (tumour-agnostic FDA [Food and Drug Administration] approval, 2017); then germline Lynch testing.
- Tissue preferred; ctDNA alternative, but **CHIP (clonal haematopoiesis of indeterminate potential)** gives false calls.

### Indications: tissue classifiers
- **Low and favourable-intermediate risk:** active surveillance versus treatment; surveillance intensity. Never the sole basis.
- **Intermediate and high risk:** metastasis risk guides treatment intensity.
- **After prostatectomy:** high Decipher score may favour adjuvant or early salvage radiotherapy (retrospective evidence).
- **Limits:** tumour heterogeneity, retrospective data, cost.

- **Also know:** ConfirmMDx methylation before repeat biopsy; polygenic risk scores.

**Diagram to draw:** flowchart: metastatic → germline + somatic; localised high-risk or family history → germline; localised low or intermediate → tissue classifier.

> **Key points to remember:**
> - Germline: all metastatic, high-risk, family history, Ashkenazi.
> - Somatic: HRR → PARP inhibitors; MSI-H → pembrolizumab.
> - BRCA2: aggressive; avoid casual active surveillance.
> - Decipher, Oncotype GPS, Prolaris: prognosis only.

## Rapid Revision Sheet – Section 17 · Prostate Cancer

*All the key points from this section in one place. Read this in the last 30 minutes before the exam.*

#### Role and impact of Radiotherapy methods for management of focal and advanced carcinoma prostate? (LAQ)

- α/β ~1.5 Gy: basis of 60 Gy/20 (CHHiP) and SBRT 36.25 Gy/5.
- ADT 4–6 months unfavourable intermediate; 18–36 months high risk.
- Early salvage at PSA 0.1–0.2 = adjuvant (RADICALS, RAVES, GETUG-AFU 17).
- STAMPEDE H: prostate RT for low-volume M1; ALSYMPCA, VISION for mCRPC.

#### Discuss advances in management of CA prostate (LAQ)

- PROMIS and PRECISION: MRI before biopsy finds more significant cancer and avoids biopsies.
- proPSMA: PSMA PET accuracy 92% vs 65%.
- ProtecT: no mortality difference at 15 years; AS is standard for GG1.
- VISION (Lu-PSMA, HR 0.62); PEACE-1 and ARASENS triplets; EMBARK.

#### Cryotherapy for carcinoma prostate (SAQ)

- Argon freezes, helium thaws; −40 °C, two cycles, urethral warmer.
- Main role is salvage: PSA <10, doubling time ≥16 months.
- Whole-gland ED near universal; fistula ≤0.8% focal, up to 3.4% salvage.

#### Saturation biopsies of prostate (SAQ)

- Saturation = >12 cores (18–21 or more); 5-mm template mapping.
- No gain at initial biopsy; used after negatives.
- PROMIS: template mapping as reference; mpMRI sensitivity 93%.

#### Prostate cancer prevention trials (SAQ)

- PCPT ↓24.8%; REDUCE ↓22.8%; both ↑ high grade.
- FDA warning; no approved preventive agent.
- SELECT negative; vitamin E alone harmful.

#### GnRH (LHRH) analogues: agonists and antagonists (SAQ)

- Agonist: surge, then down-regulation; continuous stimulation → down-regulation → castration in 2–4 weeks.
- Flare 10–20 days; cover flare with antiandrogen 21–28 days.
- Antagonist: no flare; degarelix castrate by day 3; relugolix oral (HERO, fewer MACE).
- Castrate <50 ng/dL; aim <20 ng/dL.

#### Combined Androgen Blockade. (SAQ)

- CAB = castration + non-steroidal antiandrogen (adrenal androgens).
- PCTCG: ~2–3% 5-year survival gain only.
- Main use now: flare cover for 21–28 days.
- ARPIs (androgen receptor pathway inhibitors) or triplets now standard in mHSPC.

#### PSMA PET Ct scan (SAQ)

- proPSMA: accuracy 92% vs 65%.
- BCR: about half positive below PSA 0.5 ng/mL.
- Nodal sensitivity 30–40%: negative scan does not exclude nodes.
- VISION: ¹⁷⁷Lu-PSMA-617 improves OS in mCRPC.

#### Methods to predict prognosis of patient of Cancer prostate (SAQ)

- Grade group is the strongest predictor.
- D'Amico, NCCN and CAPRA combine PSA, grade and stage.
- Decipher, Oncotype, Prolaris add genomic risk.

#### PARP inhibitors in Prostate cancer. (SAQ)

- Synthetic lethality; BRCA2 gains most, ATM least.
- PROfound: olaparib, PFS 7.4 vs 3.6 months.
- Combinations: PROpel, MAGNITUDE, TALAPRO-2.

#### PI-RADS and MRI–ultrasound fusion biopsy (SAQ)

- Peripheral zone by DWI, transition zone by T2W; DCE only lifts peripheral 3 to 4.
- Fusion: co-registration with elastic deformation.
- Cognitive, fusion, in-bore: similar yield.
- PROMIS and PRECISION: MRI before biopsy.

#### Indications of genomic testing in carcinoma prostate (SAQ)

- Germline: all metastatic, high-risk, family history, Ashkenazi.
- Somatic: HRR → PARP inhibitors; MSI-H → pembrolizumab.
- BRCA2: aggressive; avoid casual active surveillance.
- Decipher, Oncotype GPS, Prolaris: prognosis only.
